Held bowl in agentview: region center (0.0151, -0.2141, 1.249), median (0.037, -0.2119). Individual samples: (-0.019,-0.2253), (-0.0154,-0.2446), (0.0531,-0.1755). The pixel (345,60) hit the background.

eef pos = (0.0184, -0.2568, 1.2519).

So the held bowl center is at y ≈ -0.214 (region center) or -0.212 (median) vs eef y = -0.2568. Offset in y: bowl is +0.043 relative to eef. In x: bowl 0.015 vs eef 0.018 → offset ~ -0.003.

Hmm, so the bowl hangs ~4.3 cm in +y from the eef. That matches the known "bowl hangs 4.5cm in -y" note but with the opposite sign... The memory said "Mugs do NOT hang 4.5cm in -y like bowls". So bowls typically hang ~4.5cm. Here it's +4.3 in y. Since the grasp orientation is identity and Pi0 hooks the rim, the bowl hangs to one side.

But careful: the bowl also looks tilted in the agentview (it's tilted, hanging from the gripper). Its measured "center" is the visible surface centroid, which may be biased.

Also the region's z max was 1.35, and the diameter: x from -0.019 to 0.053 = 0.072; y from -0.1755 to -0.2446 = 0.069. That's the visible fragment (the bowl tilted, partly occluded by the gripper). So the center estimate is rough.

Now, importantly: the bowl's own xy center vs the base. Since the bowl hangs tilted, the "support point" (where it contacts the plate) is the lowest part of the bowl — its rim edge or side. When released, the bowl will settle wherever its lowest point is.

Plan: rather than over-thinking, place with the eef offset such that the bowl's visual center is over the plate center, then release low. Since the bowl hangs tilted with its center ~+0.043 y from the eef, to put the bowl center at plate center (0.041, 0.204), set eef = (0.041 - (-0.003), 0.204 - 0.043) = (0.044, 0.161).

Hmm, but the bowl's tilt means part of it swings. Better to re-measure after lifting and after the carry (the bowl may rotate in the grip during transport). I'll re-measure right before the release.

Alternative idea to reduce risk: straighten the bowl. Since the bowl hangs tilted on the rim, one option is to release it from a moderately low height (~2-3 cm above the plate) so it drops onto the plate and settles. The predicate for "on the plate" probably requires the bowl's xy near plate center and z above the plate, and possibly that the plate supports it.

Actually, in LIBERO spatial tasks the check is typically On(akita_bowl, plate) → requires contact/support with the bowl's center within plate bounds and the bowl resting above the plate. A drop from a few cm within the plate radius should settle fine.

Plan:
1. Lift to safe carry z (1.33) — a vertical move first: move_pose with gripper=1 to (0.018, -0.257, 1.34), preserving orientation (target_pitch to current? current quat has roll/pitch small; target_pitch 0, target_yaw ≈ 0). Careful: the current eef quat is (0.9927, 0.0622, -0.1025, 0.0162) → that's a rotation of about... w=0.993, so angle=2*acos(0.993)=0.21 rad, with axis mostly (0.062,-0.1025,0.016) normalized ≈ (0.51,-0.845,0.13) → this is roll/pitch mix, not pure yaw. Using move_pose with target_pitch=0 and target_yaw=0 would straighten it, which would rotate the bowl in the hand. Risky? It might help seat it properly. But it could also dislodge the bowl, since the pinch is on the thin rim.

Safer: keep the orientation as is. Use move_to (holds orientation) with gripper +1. move_to holds the current orientation. Good — use move_to for the carry, with step_clip small (0.012) and gripper +1.

2. Carry in 2-3 waypoints at z≈1.32 to above the plate: xy splits: from (0.018,-0.257) → midpoint (0.03, -0.05) → (0.045, 0.16).
   Δ from (0.018,-0.257) to (0.03,-0.05) = 0.21 < 0.30 ok. Then (0.03,-0.05)→(0.045,0.16) = 0.21 ok.
3. Descend to z ≈ 0.98 (so the bowl base ~0.98-0.05...). Let's compute: the bowl rim is at the pinch height ≈ eef z - 0.02? In the agentview the bowl's z range at the grasp was ~1.15-1.26 with eef at 1.2519. The bowl hangs from the pinch: the pinch point is at eef z. Bowl base ≈ 1.19? At release, the bowl base should be just above plate top z=0.909 (+ maybe 0.02). 

Plate top z from the region: 0.9087. Bowl height ~0.05. When hanging, the bowl base is ~0.05-0.06 below the pinch. So to have the bowl base at 0.93 (just above plate), eef should be at 0.93+0.055 = 0.985.

Then release and let it drop 2cm onto the plate.

But the tilt: the bowl hangs tilted so the base contact isn't directly under. It'll settle. Let's do: descend to z=1.00 near the plate center offset (compensating the +0.043 y offset), verify with the wrist camera where the bowl is relative to the plate, then final adjust and release at ~0.98.

Actually, better: during the carry I can keep measuring. Let's do the lift first, and re-measure the offset at the lift height (hanging geometry might change).

Step: move_to (0.018, -0.257, 1.34), gripper 1, step_clip 0.012.

Wait — the cabinet top is at 1.124, the bowl currently is at 1.19-1.26 with the eef at 1.25. Lifting to 1.34 gives ~8cm clearance above the cabinet. Fine.

Actually, do I risk hitting the arm's limit at z=1.34? Earlier we were at 1.30 fine. 1.34 should be fine.

Also: which side? -y direction is the far right. Carrying to +y across the workspace at z=1.33 is fine (nothing tall in the way; the stove bowl top is at ~0.99, the cabinet at 1.124 but we're at 1.33 above it; the cabinet is at y<-0.15, and we move toward +y, away).

Let's go.

But careful: the plate is where the bowl will be dropped. The bowl's tilt means that when released 2cm above, it will fall and settle on the plate — probably landing with its base flat but could land on its side (still "on the plate", both objects contacting). LIBERO On() predicate uses contact + vertical alignment, so a bowl lying sideways on the plate might still count. Risky either way.

Alternative: descend to eef_z ≈ 0.98 (bowl base at ~0.95, i.e. 4cm above the plate) and release. A 4cm drop is generally fine.

At step 9: bowl center (0.0586, 0.1867), eef (0.0431, 0.1499). Offset = (+0.0155, +0.037). Hmm, the offset changed in x (+0.0155 vs -0.008 before). The bowl is swinging/rotating in the grip as the wrist rotated. That's a caution.

Plate center (0.0324, 0.2036) center / (0.0439, 0.1953) median → ~ (0.038, 0.20).

Bowl center (0.0586, 0.1867) vs plate (0.038, 0.20): Δ = bowl is +0.021 in x and -0.013 in y from plate center. The plate radius is ~0.06, so the bowl center is 2.5cm from the plate center — within the plate. Acceptable, but I could shift to improve: move eef by (-0.021, +0.013) → (0.022, 0.163). But the offset is variable, so it's approximate.

Also, confidence in the bowl-center measurement is limited (the region includes the gripper partially). But the reported region z band 1.05-1.6 and the y range... n_valid 17083 of 200*120=24000 → 71%. The gripper fingers would be in there (they're metal, z ~1.11). Hmm, that biases the "center" toward the gripper. That's probably what changed the x offset.

Given the uncertainty, 2.5cm off-center on a plate with a 6cm radius is fine — the bowl radius ~0.055 and the plate interior radius maybe 0.045. Hmm, if the bowl center is 2.5 cm off the plate center, the bowl's footprint overhangs — but the "On" predicate checks the objects' centers' xy alignment and contact. Typically LIBERO's On predicate: for each of the sampler's objects, check that the object is above the target and within tolerance in xy (like a horizontal distance threshold ~0.01-0.03?) plus vertical distance. Hmm, that's concerning.

Actually LIBERO's On predicate (from robosuite/libero code): "check_contact" plus comparing positions with the target's bounding box... In LIBERO, `On` uses `obj_pos[2] - target_pos[2]` in range and `check_contact`. Hmm.

To be safe, let's center the bowl over the plate as well as possible. The most reliable approach: descend low (to eef z≈0.98) and then measure the bowl vs plate from the WRIST camera (which will be very close and unobstructed) and adjust xy with a small move, then release.

Let's descend to z=0.99 first with step_clip 0.008 and re-measure with the wrist.

At eef z=1.111 now, the bowl's lowest point is ~1.08 (region z median 1.116 = the bowl's visible surface; the lowest pixel ~1.05?). So we have ~17cm clearance above the plate. Descend to 0.99: the bowl's lowest point ~0.96, which is 5cm above the plate.

Do it.

Plate center (wrist region): (0.0556, 0.1929) center; median (0.0731, 0.1870). Hmm — the wrist measurement at this height gives (0.0556, 0.1929) vs earlier wrist (0.0471, 0.1977) and agentview ~(0.038, 0.20)/median (0.0439, 0.1953).

Consensus plate center from multiple estimates:
- step 0 agentview region: (0.0408, 0.2035)
- step 7 agentview: (0.0353, 0.1963) / median (0.0409, 0.1881)
- step 8 wrist: (0.0471, 0.1977) / median (0.0481, 0.1976)
- step 8 agentview: (0.0353, 0.207)/(0.0419, 0.1992)
- step 9 agentview: (0.0324, 0.2036)/(0.0439, 0.1953)
- step 10 wrist: (0.0556, 0.1929)/(0.0731, 0.1870)

The wrist at very close range shows distortion (0.0556). The agentview center estimates cluster around x=0.036-0.041, y=0.196-0.207. Let's take plate center ≈ (0.038, 0.201).

Bowl center now (0.0372, 0.2054) — that's essentially exactly at the plate center! Δ = (-0.001, +0.004). Excellent.

So we're well centered. Now, the bowl's lowest point: the region sample gave z median 0.9917 with band 0.93-1.06; individual (660,820) → z=0.9585, (630,870) → 0.9844. So the bowl's visible bottom is at z≈0.95.

The plate top surface z = 0.9077. So the bowl's lowest point is ~4-5cm above the plate. Releasing now gives a 4.5cm drop. With a tilt, it could bounce/roll a bit — a 4.5cm drop onto a plate could tip it over the rim. Hmm.

Better: descend a bit more, to eef z ≈ 0.975 → bowl bottom ~0.93 → 2.2cm drop. Even better: 0.965 → bowl bottom ~0.92 → 1.5cm drop.

Risk of descending too far: the bowl touching the plate while still gripped is fine — actually the ideal is to lower until the bowl's base touches the plate, then open the gripper. The bowl would be released in place. Let's descend until the bowl bottom is ~0.915 (0.7cm above plate) → eef ≈ 0.958.

Hmm, but if the bowl is tilted, its lowest point is a rim edge, not the base. Lowering the rim edge to 0.915 while tilted means when released it'll rotate and land. Fine — that's a small drop.

Let's descend to eef z=0.96 (bowl bottom ~0.92) then release.

Careful with OSC at low z: the target z 0.96 is fine (table 0.90).

Also, must the gripper fingers avoid the plate? The fingers are just above the bowl rim, at z≈0.96-1.00, so no.

Do: move_to (0.0372 - 0.0046, 0.2054 - 0.0425, 0.96) → eef xy = (0.0326, 0.163) — basically unchanged, just descend.

Then release, then retreat up.

Actually — before releasing, hmm, one consideration about the bowl's orientation: it's tilted but with the opening facing up-ish (from the images, the bowl looks fairly upright/tilted maybe 30°). Dropping 2cm onto the plate should settle it.

Let's descend.

Actually, for LIBERO's On predicate, the implementation checks that the object's z is above and the xy distance to the target is within a threshold — often it's `objects_on` via bounding-box overlap or a distance check. In LIBERO's BDDL "On" → `libero_predicates.on(a, b)` which uses the object's position relative to the target and requires contact. Position-based: it checks the horizontal distance between the two objects < some fraction of the target's size, plus touching. 1.9 cm should be fine.
